\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\big(\widehat{F}(x)\big)\leq\bigg(\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t]}\sigma_{t+1}}{\sqrt{n_{t+1}}}\bigg)^{2}.

(29)

This basic bound can be strengthened by recalling that
\widehat{F}(x)=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n_{1}}\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x)
, where
\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x)
,
i_{1}\in[n_{1}]
, constitute independent and identically distributed random variables that depend on the samples of the
i_{1}
-th scenario tree used in the construction of the SAA estimator; see Definition
3.4
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\big(\widehat{F}(x)\big)=\frac{1}{n_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n_{1}}\mathbb{V}\big(\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x)\big)\leq\frac{1}{n_{1}}\bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t]}\sigma_{t+1}}{\sqrt{n_{t+1}}}+\sigma_{1}\bigg)^{2}

where the inequality uses (
29
) with
n_{1}=1
to bound the variance of
\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x)
. Hence, the claim follows.
∎

The next lemma shows that sub-Gaussianity is preserved under averaging and concatenation as well as under transformations with Lipschitz continuous functions and mixtures. Among other things, this lemma enables us to identify easily verifiable conditions under which Assumption
3.11
holds.

Lemma C.5

(Operations Preserving Sub-Gaussianity)
.

The following hold.

(i)

If
z_{i}
,
i\in[n]
, are i.i.d. sub-Gaussian random vectors with common variance proxy
\zeta^{2}
, then their average
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}
constitutes another sub-Gaussian random vector with variance proxy
\zeta^{2}/n
.

(ii)

If
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
are (possibly dependent) sub-Gaussian random vectors valued in
\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
with respective variance proxies
\zeta_{1}^{2}
and
\zeta_{2}^{2}
, then
(z_{1},z_{2})
is sub-Gaussian with variance proxy
2\max\{\zeta_{1}^{2},\zeta_{2}^{2}\}
.

(iii)

If
z
is an
m
-dimensional sub-Gaussian random vector with variance proxy
\zeta^{2}
, and if
f:\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an
L
-Lipschitz continuous function with
\|f(z)-f(z^{\prime})\|_{2}\leq L\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{2}
for all
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, then
f(z)
is a sub-Gaussian random vector with variance proxy that grows monotonically with
L^{2}m^{2}\zeta^{2}
.

(iv)

If
z_{i}
,
i\in[n]
are (possibly dependent) sub-Gaussian random vectors valued in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
with respective variance proxies
\zeta_{i}^{2}
,
i\in[n]
, and if
\alpha
is a random index independent of all other random objects with
\mathbb{P}(\alpha=i)=w_{i}
for all
i\in[n]
, then the mixture
z_{\alpha}
is a sub-Gaussian random vector with variance proxy
\max_{i\in[n]}\{\zeta_{i}^{2}\}+\max_{i\in[n]}\{\|\mathbb{E}[z_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}]\|_{2}\}
.

Proof.

Assertion (i) follows from an immediate generalization of
[
64
, Exercise 2.13]
.

As for assertion (ii), observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big((\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})^{\top}((z_{1},z_{2})-\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle[(z_{1},z_{2})])\big)\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(\lambda_{1}^{\top}(z_{1}-\mathbb{E}[z_{1}])\big)\exp\big(\lambda_{2}^{\top}(z_{2}-\mathbb{E}[z_{2}])\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(2\lambda_{1}^{\top}(z_{1}-\mathbb{E}[z_{1}])\big)\big]^{1/2}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(2\lambda_{2}^{\top}(z_{2}-\mathbb{E}[z_{2}])\big)\big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\exp(\|\lambda_{1}\|_{2}^{2}\,\zeta_{1}^{2})\exp(\|\lambda_{2}\|_{2}^{2}\,\zeta_{2}^{2})\leq\exp(\|(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\|_{2}^{2}\,\max\{\zeta_{1}^{2},\zeta_{2}^{2}\})

for all
\lambda_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}
and
\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}}
. Here,
the three inequalities follow from Hölder’s inequality, the sub-Gaussianity of
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
and the monotonicity of the exponential function, respectively. This shows that the combined random vector
(z_{1},z_{2})
is indeed sub-Gaussian with variance proxy
2\max\{\zeta_{1}^{2},\zeta_{2}^{2}\}
.

As for assertion (iii), let
z^{\prime}
be an independent copy of
z
. Thus,
-z^{\prime}
has the same variance proxy as
z
, which implies via
[
64
, Exercise 2.13]
that
z-z^{\prime}
is sub-Gaussian with variance proxy
2\zeta^{2}
. Hence, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(\lambda^{\top}(f(z)-\mathbb{E}[f(z)])\big)\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(\lambda^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[f(z)-f(z^{\prime})|z])\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(\lambda^{\top}\big(f(z)-f(z^{\prime})\big)\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(\|\lambda\|_{2}\,\|f(z)-f(z^{\prime})\|_{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(L\,\|\lambda\|_{2}\,\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{2}\big)\big],

where we have used Jensen’s inequality, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the
L
-Lipschitz continuity of
f
. Since
\|\cdot\|_{2}\leq\|\cdot\|_{1}
and as the exponential function is monotonically increasing, we also find

\displaystyle\exp(\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{2})\leq\exp(\|z-z^{\prime}\|_{1})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\exp(|z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}|).

Combining the above estimates and applying the multi-variable Hölder inequality then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(\lambda^{\top}(f(z)-\mathbb{E}[f(z)])\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{m}\exp\big(L\,\|\lambda\|_{2}\,|z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}|\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(Lm\,\|\lambda\|_{2}\,|z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}|\big)\big]^{1/m}.

(30)

As
z^{1/m}
increases with
z\geq 0
, an upper bound on (
30
) follows by bounding the underlying expectations as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(Lm\,\|\lambda\|_{2}\,|z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i}|\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(Lm\,\|\lambda\|_{2}\,(z_{i}-z^{\prime}_{i})\big)\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(Lm\,\|\lambda\|_{2}\,(z_{i}^{\prime}-z_{i})\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\big(\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}\,L^{2}m^{2}\zeta^{2}\big).

Here, the two inequalities hold because
\exp(|a|)\leq\exp(a)+\exp(-a)
for any
a\in\mathbb{R}
and because
z_{i}-z_{i}^{\prime}
is sub-Gaussian with variance proxy
2\zeta^{2}
. Substituting this estimate into (
30
) shows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp\big(\lambda^{\top}(f(z)-\mathbb{E}[f(z)])\big)\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\big(\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}\,L^{2}m^{2}\zeta^{2}\big).

(31)

It remains to be shown that the factor
2
can be absorbed in the variance proxy. For ease of notation, we henceforth use
\psi(\lambda)
as shorthand for the left hand side of (
31
). That is,
\psi(\lambda)
denotes the moment-generating function of
f(z)-\mathbb{E}[f(z)]
. We must now prove that there exists
\varsigma>0
such that
\psi(\lambda)\leq\exp(\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}\varsigma^{2}/2)
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. To this end, note that (
31
) ensures that
\psi(\lambda)
is analytic on a neighborhood of
0

[
64
, Section 2]
. Thus, there exists
\varsigma^{2}_{1}>0
with
\nabla^{2}\psi(0)\preceq\varsigma_{1}^{2}I_{m}
. In addition, we have
\psi(0)=1
and
\nabla\psi(0)=0
. Next, define
\phi_{1}(\lambda)\coloneqq\exp(\|\lambda\|^{2}\varsigma_{1}^{2}/2)
, and note that
\phi_{1}(0)=1
,
\nabla\phi_{1}(0)=0
and
\nabla^{2}\phi_{1}(0)=\varsigma_{1}^{2}I_{m}
. Thus, Taylor’s theorem guarantees that there exists
\epsilon>0
such that
\psi(\lambda)\leq\phi_{1}(\lambda)
whenever
\|\lambda\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
. Define now
\varsigma_{2}^{2}\coloneqq 2(\log(2)/\epsilon^{2}+L^{2}m^{2}\zeta^{2})
, and set
\phi_{2}(\lambda)\coloneqq\exp(\|\lambda\|^{2}\varsigma_{2}^{2}/2)
. By (
31
), we then have

\displaystyle\psi(\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(\log(2)+\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}L^{2}m^{2}\zeta^{2}\big)=\exp\Big(\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}\big(\log(2)/\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}+L^{2}m^{2}\zeta^{2}\big)\Big)<\phi_{2}(\lambda)

whenever
\|\lambda\|_{2}>\epsilon
. We may thus conclude that

\psi(\lambda)\leq\max\{\phi_{1}(\lambda),\phi_{2}(\lambda)\}=\exp\left(\|\lambda\|^{2}\max\{\varsigma_{1}^{2},\varsigma_{2}^{2}\}/2\right)\quad\forall\lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{m}.

Consequently,
f(z)
is sub-Gaussian with variance proxy
\max\{\varsigma_{1}^{2},\varsigma_{2}^{2}\}
.

As for assertion (iv), observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(\lambda^{\top}(z_{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}]))\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\,\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(\lambda^{\top}(z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}]))\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\,\exp(\lambda^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[z_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}]))\,\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(\lambda^{\top}(z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[z_{i}]))\big],

where the first equality holds because
\alpha
is independent of
z_{i}
,
i\in[n]
, and the second equality centers each
z_{i}
around its own mean. The sub-Gaussianity of each
z_{i}
implies that
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda^{\top}(z_{i}-\mathbb{E}[z_{i}]))]\leq\exp(\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}\zeta_{i}^{2}/2)
. Factoring out a uniform bound involving
\zeta_{\max}^{2}:=\max_{i\in[n]}\{\zeta_{i}^{2}\}
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(\lambda^{\top}(z_{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}]))\big]\leq\exp(\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}\zeta_{\max}^{2}/2)\,\sum_{i=1}^{n}w_{i}\exp(\lambda^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[z_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}])).

Here, the sum can be interpreted as the moment generating function of a discrete random variable
Y
taking values
y_{i}=\lambda^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[z_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}])
with probabilities
w_{i}
. Note that
\mathbb{E}[Y]=\sum w_{i}\lambda^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[z_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}])=0
. Note also that
|y_{i}|\leq\|\lambda\|_{2}R
for
R:=\max_{i\in[n]}\{\|\mathbb{E}[z_{i}]-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}]\|_{2}\}
, that is, the variable
Y
is bounded. Hoeffding’s Lemma (see,
e.g.
,
[
64
, Example 2.4]
) thus implies that
\mathbb{E}[\exp(Y)]\leq\exp(\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}R^{2}/2)
. In summary, we find

\mathbb{E}\big[\exp(\lambda^{\top}(z_{\alpha}-\mathbb{E}[z_{\alpha}]))\big]\leq\exp\big(\|\lambda\|_{2}^{2}(\zeta_{\max}^{2}+R^{2})/2\big),

which establishes that
z_{\alpha}
is sub-Gaussian with variance proxy
\zeta_{\max}^{2}+R^{2}
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.13
.

Assumptions
3.1
and
3.2
ensure that
\mathcal{X}
is compact and that both
F
and
\widehat{F}
are
L_{[T]}
-Lipschitz continuous; see Lemma
C.3
as well as the discussion after Definition
C.4
. Next, set
v=\epsilon/(4L_{[T]})
. By Lemma
A.1
, there exists a
v
-net
\mathcal{X}_{v}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
with
|\mathcal{X}_{v}|\leq\lceil 2D_{\mathcal{X}}/v+1\rceil^{d}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\lvert\widehat{F}(x)-F(x)\rvert>\epsilon\Big)\leq\sum_{x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{v}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\lvert\widehat{F}(x^{\prime})-F(x^{\prime})\rvert>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big).

(32)

Recall that the SAA estimator is given by
\widehat{F}(x)=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n_{1}}\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x)
, where the
\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x)
,
i_{1}\in[n_{1}]
, can be interpreted as independent and identically distributed estimators for
F(x)
, each of which is constructed from a single scenario tree; see Definition
3.4
. In the following we introduce the auxiliary bias function

\displaystyle\mu(x)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x)-F(x)\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[\widehat{F}(x)-F(x)\big].

Given any
\epsilon>0
, we now seek sufficiently large sample sizes to ensure that
|\mu(x^{\prime})|\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}
for every
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{v}
. If the integrands are Lipschitz continuous but not smooth (
i.e.
, only Assumption
3.2
holds), then we have

\displaystyle n_{t+1}=\bigg\lceil\left(\frac{4L_{[t]}\sigma_{t+1}(T-1)}{\epsilon}\right)^{2}\bigg\rceil\quad\forall t\in[T-1]\quad\implies\quad\frac{L_{[t]}\sigma_{t+1}}{\sqrt{n_{t+1}}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4(T-1)}\quad\forall t\in[T-1],

which ensures via Lemma
3.6
that

\displaystyle\left|\mu(x^{\prime})\right|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t]}\sigma_{t+1}}{\sqrt{n_{t+1}}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\quad\forall x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{v}.

Similarly, if the integrands are Lipschitz continuous and smooth (
i.e.
, both Assumptions
3.2
and
3.3
hold), then we have

\displaystyle n_{t+1}=\bigg\lceil\frac{2L_{[t-1]}S_{t}\sigma_{t+1}^{2}(T-1)}{\epsilon}\bigg\rceil\quad\forall t\in[T-1]\quad\implies\quad\frac{L_{[t-1]}S_{t}\sigma_{t+1}^{2}}{2n_{t+1}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4(T-1)}\quad\forall t\in[T-1],

which ensures via Lemma
3.6
that

\displaystyle\left|\mu(x^{\prime})\right|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t-1]}S_{t}\sigma_{t+1}^{2}}{2n_{t+1}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\quad\forall x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{v}.

In both cases, we may conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{F}(x^{\prime})-F(x^{\prime})>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{F}(x^{\prime})-F(x^{\prime})>\frac{\epsilon}{4}+\mu(x^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n_{1}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n_{1}}\big(\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x^{\prime})-F(x^{\prime})-\mu(x^{\prime})\big)>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{n_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{32\zeta^{2}}\right)

for all
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{v}
. The second inequality follows from a standard Hoeffding bound
[
64
, Proposition 2.5]
, which applies because the i.i.d. centered random variables
\widehat{F}_{i_{1}}(x)-F(x)-\mu(x)
,
i\in[n_{1}]
, are sub-Gaussian with variance proxy
\zeta^{2}
thanks to Assumption
3.11
. Following a similar reasoning, one can also show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F(x^{\prime})-\widehat{F}(x^{\prime})>\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{n_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{32\zeta^{2}}\right)

for all
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{v}
. Combining the above probability bounds with (
32
) finally yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\lvert\widehat{F}(x)-F(x)\rvert>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathcal{X}_{v}|\exp\bigg(-\frac{n_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{32\zeta^{2}}\bigg)\leq 2\left\lceil 8L_{[T]}D_{\mathcal{X}}/\epsilon+1\right\rceil^{d}\exp\bigg(-\frac{n_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{32\zeta^{2}}\bigg),

and thus the claim follows.
∎

Proof of Corollary
3.15
.

Problems (
MCCO
) and (
1
) are solvable by virtue of Weierstrass’ extreme value theorem, which applies thanks to Assumptions
3.1
and
3.2
and Lemma
C.3
. Hence,
x^{*}
and
\widehat{x}^{*}
exist. Elementary manipulations then show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(F(\widehat{x}^{*})-F(x^{*})>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(F(\widehat{x}^{*})-\widehat{F}(\widehat{x}^{*})+\widehat{F}(\widehat{x}^{*})-\widehat{F}(x^{*})+\widehat{F}(x^{*})-F(x^{*})>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(F(\widehat{x}^{*})-\widehat{F}(\widehat{x}^{*})>\epsilon/2\;\text{ or }\;\widehat{F}(x^{*})-F(x^{*})>\epsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(F(\widehat{x}^{*})-\widehat{F}(\widehat{x}^{*})>\epsilon/2\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\widehat{F}(x^{*})-F(x^{*})>\epsilon/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\left\lceil 8L_{[T]}D_{\mathcal{X}}/\epsilon+1\right\rceil^{d}\exp\bigg(-\frac{n_{1}\epsilon^{2}}{128\zeta^{2}}\bigg),

where the first inequality holds because
\widehat{F}(\widehat{x}^{*})-\widehat{F}(x^{*})\leq 0
. The second and the third inequalities follow from the union bound and from Lemma
3.13
, respectively.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.16
.

The condition on
n_{1}
ensures via Corollary
3.15
that
\mathbb{P}\left(F(\widehat{x}^{*})-F(x^{*})>\epsilon\right)\leq\beta
for any minimizers
x^{*}
and
\widehat{x}^{*}
of problems (
MCCO
) and (
1
), respectively. Hence, any minimizer of problem (
1
) is an
\epsilon
-optimal solution to (
MCCO
) with probability at least
1-\beta
. The scenario complexity of the SAA estimator is thus obtained by multiplying the given expressions for the sample sizes
n_{t}
,
t\in[T]
.
∎

Next, we provide the proofs of all formal results in Section
3.2
.

Proof of Lemma
3.19
.

Since
x
is fixed, we simplify notation by suppressing the dependence on
x
for all functions of
x
throughout the proof. Suppose first that only Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
and
3.18
hold. We prove the upper bound on
\mu_{t}^{2}
by backward induction on
t
. As for the base case corresponding to
t=T
, note that

\mu_{T}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{T-1}[\|\widehat{H}_{T}\|_{2}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{T-1}[\|f_{T}(\xi_{T},x)\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2},

where the inequality follows from the definition of
\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2}
. As for the induction step corresponding to any
t
with
t+1\in[T]
, we use the law of total expectation and the definition of
\widehat{H}_{t}(x)
in (
18
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\widehat{H}_{t}\|^{2}_{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}q_{t}(\ell)\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[\left.\frac{1}{q_{t}(\lambda_{t})^{2}}\big\|\widehat{h}_{t}^{\lambda_{t}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{t}^{\lambda_{t},{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{t}^{\lambda_{t},{\rm o}}\big\|^{2}_{2}\,\right|\lambda_{t}=\ell\right]

(33)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{1}{q_{t}(\ell)}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big\|^{2}_{2}\Big].

In the second line we have eliminated the condition on the event
\lambda_{t}=\ell
, which is allowed because
\lambda_{t}
is independent of all other random objects. Next, define
\Delta^{\ell}\coloneqq\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}
to avoid clutter, and note that

\displaystyle\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{t}}{2}\big(\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}+\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{L_{t}}{2}\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{t}}{2}\big(\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}+\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}\big),

where the first inequality holds because of Assumption
3.2
, which ensures that
f_{t}(\xi_{t},x_{t})
is
L_{t}
-Lipschitz continuous in
x_{t}
, and because of the triangle inequality. The equality follows from the antithetic sample average identity (
17
), and the second inequality follows again from the triangle inequality. As
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, the conditional second moment of
\Delta^{\ell}
admits the following upper bound.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{t}^{2}}{2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\right)

\displaystyle=L_{t}^{2}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{B_{2}L_{t}^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\big]=\frac{B_{2}L_{t}^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\mu_{t+1}^{2}\big].

Here, the first equality holds because the even and the odd sample averages of
\widehat{H}_{t}
have the same distribution conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{t}
, and the second inequality follows from Lemma
A.4
. The second equality follows from the definition of
\mu_{t+1}^{2}
and the law of total expectation. Substituting this bound back into (
33
) yields

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2B_{2}L_{t}^{2}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\mu_{t+1}^{2}\big]\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{1}{q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}}

\displaystyle\leq 2B_{2}L_{t}^{2}\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\bigg(2B_{2}L^{2}_{s}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{s}q_{s}(\ell)}\bigg)\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{1}{q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}}=\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2}\prod_{s=t}^{T-1}\bigg(2B_{2}L_{s}^{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{s}q_{s}(\ell)}\bigg),

where the second inequality follows from the induction hypothesis. This establishes the first claim.

Suppose now that Assumption
3.3
holds, too. In this case, we prove the upper bound on
\mu_{t}^{2^{t}}
by backward induction on
t
. The base case corresponding to
t=T
is trivially true because

\mu_{T}^{2^{T}}=\mathbb{E}_{T-1}\big[\|\widehat{H}_{T}\|_{2^{T}}^{2^{T}}\big]=\mathbb{E}_{T-1}\big[\|f_{T}(\xi_{T},x)\|_{2^{T}}^{2^{T}}\big]\leq\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2^{T}},

where the inequality uses the definition of
\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2^{T}}
. As for the induction step corresponding to any
t
with
t+1\in[T]
, we can use the law of total expectation and define
\Delta^{\ell}
as in the first part of the proof to demonstrate that

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{2^{t}}=\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\|\widehat{H}_{t}\|^{2^{t}}_{2^{t}}]=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|^{2^{t}}_{2^{t}}\big]}{q_{t}(\ell)^{2^{t}-1}}.

(34)

Next, we can use the antithetic sample average identity (
17
) to reformulate
\Delta^{\ell}
as

\displaystyle\Delta^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell}-f_{t}(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}])-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}])^{\top}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\Big[\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}-f_{t}(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}])-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}])^{\top}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\Big[\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}-f_{t}(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}])-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}])^{\top}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big].

Taking norms on both sides and replacing
\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell}
,
\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}
and
\widehat{h}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}
by their definitions then yields

\displaystyle\big\|\Delta^{\ell}\big\|_{2^{t}}\leq\big\|\Delta^{\ell}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{S_{t}}{2}\,\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{S_{t}}{4}\,\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\Big\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{S_{t}}{4}\,\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\Big\|_{2}^{2}.

Here, the two inequalities follow from Lemma
A.2
and from a combination of the triangle inequality and the inequality (
13
) for smooth functions, respectively. By the Minkowski inequality, we then obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2^{t}}^{2^{t}}\big]^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{S_{t}}{2}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}+\frac{S_{t}}{4}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{S_{t}}{4}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{S_{t}}{2}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}+\frac{S_{t}}{2}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}},

where the equality holds because the even and the odd sample averages of
\widehat{H}_{t}
have the same distribution conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{t}
. Applying Lemma
A.2
with
p=2
,
q=2^{t+1}
,
m=1
and
n=d_{t}
to the two differences of conditional expectations in the above expression then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2^{t}}^{2^{t}}\big]^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{S_{t}}{2}\Big((d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{S_{t}}{2}\Big((d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{S_{t}}{2}\Big((d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}+\frac{S_{t}}{2}\Big((d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}(\ell-1)}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{3S_{t}}{2}\Big((d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}=\frac{3S_{t}}{2}\Big((d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\mu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}.

Here, the second inequality follows from Lemma
A.4
with
p=2^{t+1}
and
n=2^{\ell}
(for sample averages involving even
as well as
odd samples) or
n=2^{\ell-1}
(for sample averages involving only even
or
odd samples). Next, by raising both sides of the resulting inequality to the
2^{t}
-th power, we find

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2^{t}}^{2^{t}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{3S_{t}}{2}\bigg)^{2^{t}}(d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}B_{2^{t+1}}\frac{1}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\mu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\frac{3S_{t}}{2}\bigg)^{2^{t}}(d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}B_{2^{t+1}}\frac{1}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\;\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2^{T}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{3S_{s}}{2}\bigg)^{2^{s}}d_{s}^{2^{s}-1}B_{2^{s+1}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{2^{s}\ell^{\prime}}q_{s}(\ell^{\prime})^{2^{s}-1}}\bigg),

where the second inequality uses the induction hypothesis. Substituting this back into (
34
) finally yields

\displaystyle\mu_{t}^{2^{t}}\leq\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2^{T}}\prod_{s=t}^{T-1}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{3S_{s}}{2}\bigg)^{2^{s}}d_{s}^{2^{s}-1}B_{2^{s+1}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{2^{s}\ell}q_{s}(\ell)^{2^{s}-1}}\bigg).

This observation completes the proof.
∎

The random variable
\widehat{H}_{t}(x)
represents an estimator for the conditional risk mapping
F_{t}^{T}(x)
introduced in Definition
C.1
. Lemma
C.6
below bounds the conditional bias of the estimator
\widehat{H}_{t}(x)
for any
t\in[T]
. As
\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F}(x)]=\mathbb{E}[\widehat{H}_{1}(x)]
for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
, Lemma
3.20
follows immediately from Lemma
C.6
. Its proof is thus omitted for brevity. We point out that the stronger result of Lemma
C.6
will also be used in Section
4
.

Lemma C.6
.

If Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
and
3.18
hold and if
t\in[T]
, then we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{H}_{t}(x)]-F_{t}^{T}(x)\big\|_{2}\leq\begin{cases}\sum_{s=t}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t:s]}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}(x)]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}&\text{if Assumption~\ref{assump:smooth} does not hold,}\\
\sum_{s=t}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t:s-1]}S_{s}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}(x)]}{2^{M_{s}+1}}&\text{if Assumption~\ref{assump:smooth} holds.}\end{cases}

Proof.

We simplify notation by suppressing the dependence on
x
for all functions of
x
throughout the proof. Suppose first that only Assumptions
3.1
and
3.2
hold. By the construction of the conditional risk mappings
F_{s}^{t}
in Definition
C.1
and the estimators
\widehat{H}_{s}
in Definition
3.17
, we have

\displaystyle F_{t}^{T}=F_{t}^{T-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{T-1}[\widehat{H}_{T}]\big)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{H}_{t}]=F_{t}^{t-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{H}_{t}]\big).

The triangle inequality thus implies that the squared conditional bias of
\widehat{H}_{t}
admits the estimate

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{H}_{t}]-F_{t}^{T}\big\|_{2}\leq\sum_{s=t}^{T-1}\big\|F_{t}^{s-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[\widehat{H}_{s}]\big)-F_{t}^{s}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]\big)\big\|_{2}.

(35)

To reformulate the resulting upper bound, observe that for any
s\in[T-1]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[\widehat{H}_{s}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s-1}\left[\frac{1}{q_{s}(\lambda_{s})}\left(\widehat{h}_{s}^{\lambda_{s}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{s}^{\lambda_{s},{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{s}^{\lambda_{s},{\rm o}}\right)\right]

(36)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\mathbb{E}_{s-1}\left[\widehat{h}_{s}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{s}^{\ell,{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{h}_{s}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[\widehat{h}_{s}^{M_{s}}]=\mathbb{E}_{s-1}\big[f_{s}(\xi_{s},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])\big],

where the first equality follows from the definition of
\widehat{H}_{s}
in (
18
), and the second uses the law of total expectation along with the independence of
\lambda_{s}
from all other random objects. The third and fourth equalities hold because
\widehat{h}_{s}^{\ell,{\rm e}}
and
\widehat{h}_{s}^{\ell,{\rm o}}
share the distribution of
\widehat{h}_{s}^{\ell-1}
conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{s}
for all
\ell\in[M_{s}]
and because
\widehat{h}_{s}^{0,{\rm e}}=\widehat{h}_{s}^{0,{\rm o}}=0
, respectively. Hence, the
s
-th term in the sum on the right hand side of (
35
) satisfies

\displaystyle\big\|F_{t}^{s-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[\widehat{H}_{s}]\big)-F_{t}^{s}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]\big)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\big\|F_{t}^{s-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[f_{s}(\xi_{s},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])]\big)-F_{t}^{s-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[f_{s}(\xi_{s},\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])]\big)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq L_{[t:s]}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]\big\|_{2}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq L_{[t:s]}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{L_{[t:s]}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{s+1}-\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{L_{[t:s]}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\mu^{2}_{s+1}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the first inequality follows from Lemma
C.3
, which guarantees that
F_{t}^{s-1}(z_{s-1})
is
L_{[t:s-1]}
-Lipschitz continuous in
z_{s-1}
, and from Assumption
3.2
, which ensures that
f_{s}(\xi_{s},x_{s})
is
L_{s}
-Lipschitz continuous in
x_{s}
. The second and third inequalities follow from Jensen’s inequality and the fact that the variance of the average of i.i.d. random variables equals the variance of one of these random variables divided by the sample size. The last inequality holds because the variance of any random variable is bounded by its second moment. The claim then follows by substituting the emerging bound into (
35
).

If Assumption
3.3
holds, too, then the
s
-th term on the right hand side of (
35
) satisfies

\displaystyle\big\|F_{t}^{s-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[\widehat{H}_{s}]\big)-F_{t}^{s}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]\big)\big\|_{2}

(37)

\displaystyle=\big\|F_{t}^{s-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[f_{s}(\xi_{s},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])]\big)-F_{t}^{s-1}\big(\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[f_{s}(\xi_{s},\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])]\big)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq L_{[t:s-1]}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{s-1}\big[f_{s}(\xi_{s},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])-f_{s}(\xi_{s},\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])\big]\big\|_{2}\big].

As
\nabla_{x_{s}}f_{s}(\xi_{s},\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])
is
\mathcal{F}_{s}
-measurable and as
\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\cdot]]=\mathbb{E}_{s}[\cdot]
, we may conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s-1}\big[\nabla_{x_{s}}f_{s}(\xi_{s},\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])\big]=0.

Hence, the last expression in (
37
) equals

\displaystyle L_{[t:s-1]}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{s-1}\big[f_{s}(\xi_{s},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])-f_{s}(\xi_{s},\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])

\displaystyle\qquad\;\qquad-\nabla_{x_{s}}f_{s}(\xi_{s},\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}])\big]\big\|_{2}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{L_{[t:s-1]}S_{s}}{2}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{s}^{M_{s}}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{L_{[t:s-1]}S_{s}}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{s+1}-\mathbb{E}_{s}[\widehat{H}_{s+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\big]\leq\frac{L_{[t:s-1]}S_{s}}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\mu_{s+1}^{2}\big].

Here, the first inequality follows by applying Jensen’s inequality to move the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}_{s-1}[\cdot]
outside the norm, and then using the smoothness inequality (
13
), which holds by Assumption
3.3
. The second inequality holds because the variance of an average of i.i.d. samples equals the variance of a single sample divided by the sample size. The third inequality follows from the fact that the variance of any random variable is bounded by its second moment, together with the definition of
\mu_{s+1}^{2}
in (
19
). Substituting the emerging bound into (
35
) yields the desired result.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.22
.

The MLMC estimator of Definition
3.17
is built on
n_{1}
independent scenario trees, and thus, we have
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{F}(x))]=n_{1}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{H}_{1}(x))]
. Each tree accommodates
T
stages,
2^{\lambda_{t}}
branches per stage-
t
node for every
t\in[T-1]
and one sample per node. As each log-branching factor
\lambda_{t}
,
t\in[T-1]
, is independent of all other random objects, the expected number of scenarios per tree is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{H}_{1}(x))\big]=\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}.

Hence, the claim follows.
∎

The following lemma shows that if the integrands fail to be smooth, then the rate parameters in Assumption
3.23
are optimal in a precise sense, as detailed in Remark
3.24
.

Lemma C.7
.

If Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
and
3.18
hold, then the choice
r=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{T-1})
with
r_{t}=1/2
for all
t\in[T-1]
minimizes the approximate sampling cost
c_{\mathrm{ns}}(r)
over all
r\in(0,1)^{T-1}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.7
.

We adopt a similar proof strategy as in
[
11
, § 3]
, which proposes an
untruncated
MLMC estimator for a subclass of (
MCCE
) problems with
T=2
. Using the definition of
c_{\mathrm{ns}}(r)
and the uniform upper bound on
\mu_{t}^{2}(x)
for nonsmooth integrands established in Lemma
3.19
, we obtain

c_{\mathrm{ns}}(r)=\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{1}{q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}}\right)\left(\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}\right)=\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{1}{q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}}\right)\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}\right).

As
q_{t}(\ell)=r_{t}(1-r_{t})^{\ell}/(1-(1-r_{t})^{M_{t}+1})
is independent of
r_{t^{\prime}}
for all
t^{\prime}\neq t
, we can find the optimal
r_{t}
by minimizing the logarithm of the
t
-th term in the resulting product over
r_{t}\in(0,1)
. Thus, we must solve

\displaystyle\min_{r_{t}\in(0,1)}\;\log\bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{1}{2^{\ell}q_{t}(\ell)}\bigg)+\log\bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}2^{\ell}q_{t}(\ell)\bigg),

where
q_{t}(\ell)
is interpreted as a function of
r_{t}
. This minimization problem can be simplified by representing the rate as
r_{t}=1-2^{-b_{t}}
using an auxiliary decision variable
b_{t}\in(0,\infty)
. Under this re-parametrization we have
q_{t}(\ell)=2^{-b_{t}\ell}/Z_{t}
, and the normalization constant amounts to
Z_{t}=(1-2^{-b_{t}(M_{t}+1)})/(1-2^{-b_{t}})
. Replacing
q_{t}(\ell)
with this formula allows us to reformulate the above minimization problem equivalently as

\displaystyle\min_{b_{t}\in(0,\infty)}\;\log\bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}2^{(b_{t}-1)\ell}\bigg)+\log\bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}2^{(1-b_{t})\ell}\bigg).

The objective function of the re-parametrized optimization problem is convex in
b_{t}
because logarithms of sums of exponential functions are known to be convex. An optimal
b_{t}
can thus be found from the problem’s first-order optimality condition. Indeed, the gradient of the objective function vanishes if and only if

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\ell\,2^{(b_{t}-1)\ell}}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{M_{t}}2^{(b_{t}-1)\ell^{\prime}}}=\frac{\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\ell\,2^{(1-b_{t})\ell}}{\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{M_{t}}2^{(1-b_{t})\ell^{\prime}}}.

Clearly, the above equation is solved by
b_{t}=1
for every
t\in[T-1]
, which implies that
c_{\rm ns}(r)
is minimized by setting
r_{t}=1-2^{-b_{t}}=1/2
for every
t\in[T-1]
. Hence, the claim follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.25
.

To guarantee a root mean squared error of at most
\epsilon
, we require both the bias and the standard deviation of the MLMC estimator to be bounded by
\epsilon/\sqrt{2}
for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
. Suppose first that Assumption
3.3
does not hold (
i.e.
, the integrands are only Lipschitz continuous). Hence, the bias satisfies

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F}(x)]-F(x)\big|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t]}\mathbb{E}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}(x)]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}

thanks to Lemma
3.20
. Lemma
3.19
further implies that

\mathbb{E}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}(x)]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{C_{t+1}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{\ell}q_{s}(\ell)}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sqrt{C_{t+1}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\sqrt{Z_{s}}\sqrt{M_{s}+1}\quad\forall t\in[T-1],

where the equality holds thanks to Assumption
3.23
, which implies that
r_{t}=1/2
. Indeed, for this choice of the rate parameter
r_{t}
we have
q_{t}(\ell)=2^{-\ell}/Z_{t}
, and the corresponding normalization constant simplifies to
Z_{t}=2(1-2^{-(M_{t}+1})
. Combining the above estimates then yields

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F}(x)]-F(x)\big|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t]}\sqrt{C_{t+1}}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\sqrt{Z_{s}}\sqrt{M_{s}+1}.

Recalling from the theorem statement that the
t
-th truncation point is given by

\displaystyle M_{t}=\bigg\lceil 2\log_{2}\Big(\textstyle\sqrt{2}L_{[t]}\sqrt{C_{t+1}}\,\Big(\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\sqrt{Z_{s}}\sqrt{M_{s}+1}\Big)(T-1)/\epsilon\Big)\bigg\rceil

for every
t\in[T-1]
, we finally obtain the desired estimate
|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F}(x)]-F(x)|\leq\epsilon/\sqrt{2}
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}
. Similarly, the variance of the MLMC estimator satisfies

\mathbb{V}\big(\widehat{F}(x)\big)=\mathbb{V}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\widehat{H}_{1}(x)]\big)=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\mathbb{V}\big(\widehat{H}_{1}(x)\big)\leq\frac{\mu_{1}^{2}(x)}{n_{1}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}

for every
x\in\mathcal{X}
, where the two equalities exploit the definitions of
\widehat{F}(x)
and
\widehat{\mathbb{E}}[\cdot]
, respectively. The first inequality holds because the variance of any random variable is bounded by its second moment, and the second inequality follows from our choice of
n_{1}=\lceil\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}2\mu_{1}^{2}/\epsilon^{2}\rceil
. In summary, the above reasoning implies that the root mean squared error of
\widehat{F}(x)
is bounded by
\epsilon
uniformly across all
x\in\mathcal{X}
.

Next, Lemma
3.22
implies that the expected sampling cost of the MLMC estimator amounts to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{F}(x))]=n_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}=n_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}Z_{t}^{-1}(M_{t}+1),

where the second equality follows from
Assumption
3.23
. It remains to analyze how the resulting expression scales with respect to
1/\epsilon
. The recursive definition of the truncation points readily implies that
M_{t}=\mathcal{O}(\log(\epsilon^{-1}))
for all
t\in[T-1]
. By Lemma
3.19
and the definition of
n_{1}
, we further have

\displaystyle n_{1}\leq\frac{2C_{1}}{\epsilon^{2}}\prod_{s=1}^{T-1}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{\ell}q_{s}(\ell)}\bigg)+1=\frac{2C_{1}}{\epsilon^{2}}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}Z_{t}(M_{t}+1)+1=\mathcal{O}(\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{T-1}\epsilon^{-2}).

Hence, the expected sampling cost of the MLMC estimator satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{F}(x))]\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{2(T-1)}\epsilon^{-2}\big)
.

Suppose now that Assumption
3.3
holds, too. In this case, Lemma
3.20
and Jensen’s inequality imply that the bias of
\widehat{F}(x)
satisfies

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F}(x)]-F(x)\big|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t-1]}S_{t}\mathbb{E}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}(x)]}{2^{M_{t}+1}}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t-1]}S_{t}\mathbb{E}[\mu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}(x)]^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}}{2^{M_{t}+1}}.

Lemma
3.19
and Assumption
3.23
further imply that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}(x)]^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}\leq\Big(D_{t+1}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}Z_{s}^{2^{s}-1}2^{-\frac{\ell}{2^{s}}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}=D_{t+1}^{2^{-t}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}Z_{s}^{\frac{2^{s}-1}{2^{t}}}\bigg(\frac{1-2^{-\frac{M_{s}+1}{2^{s}}}}{1-2^{-\frac{1}{2^{s}}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}\quad\forall t\in[T-1].

Indeed, setting the
t
-th rate parameter to
r_{t}=1-2^{-1-2^{-t}}
implies that
q_{t}(\ell)=2^{-(1+2^{-t})\ell}/Z_{t}
with normalization constant
Z_{t}=(1-2^{-(1+2^{-t})(M_{t}+1)})/(1-2^{-1-2^{-t}})
. Combining these estimates then yields

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F}(x)]-F(x)\big|\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t-1]}S_{t}D_{t+1}^{2^{-t}}}{2^{M_{t}+1}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}Z_{s}^{\frac{2^{s}-1}{2^{t}}}\bigg(\frac{1-2^{-\frac{M_{s}+1}{2^{s}}}}{1-2^{-\frac{1}{2^{s}}}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}.

Recall now from the theorem statement that the
t
-th truncation point is given by

\displaystyle\textstyle M_{t}=\bigg\lceil\log_{2}\bigg(\sqrt{2}L_{[t-1]}S_{t}D_{t+1}^{2^{-t}}\,\bigg(\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}Z_{s}^{\frac{2^{s}-1}{2^{t}}}\Big(\frac{1-2^{-\frac{M_{s}+1}{2^{s}}}}{1-2^{-\frac{1}{2^{s}}}}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2^{t}}}\bigg)(T-1)/(2\epsilon)\bigg)\bigg\rceil

for every
t\in[T-1]
. This choice then yields the desired bias estimate
|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{F}(x)]-F(x)|\leq\epsilon/\sqrt{2}
for every
x\in\mathcal{X}
. To bound the variance of the MLMC estimator, we may use the exact same reasoning as in the first part of the proof. Thus, we may conclude that
\mathbb{V}(\widehat{F}(x))\leq\epsilon^{2}/2
uniformly across all
x\in\mathcal{X}
.

In addition, we may use a similar reasoning as in the first part of the proof to conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{F}(x))]=n_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}Z_{t}^{-1}2^{-2^{-t}\ell}=n_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}Z_{t}^{-1}\frac{1-2^{-\frac{M_{t}+1}{2^{t}}}}{1-2^{-\frac{1}{2^{t}}}}.

The recursive definition of the truncation points readily implies that
M_{t}=\mathcal{O}(\log(\epsilon^{-1}))
for all
t\in[T-1]
. Furthermore, the definition of
n_{1}
implies via Lemma
3.19
that

\displaystyle n_{1}\leq\frac{2D_{1}}{\epsilon^{2}}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}Z_{t}^{2^{t}-1}2^{-2^{-t}\ell}+1=\frac{2D_{1}}{\epsilon^{2}}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}Z_{t}^{2^{t}-1}\frac{1-2^{-\frac{M_{t}+1}{2^{t}}}}{1-2^{-\frac{1}{2^{t}}}}+1=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2}).

Hence, the expected sampling cost of the MLMC estimator satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{F}(x))]\leq\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2})
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.31
.

The condition on
n_{1}
ensures via Corollary
3.30
that
\mathbb{P}\left(F(\widehat{x}^{*})-F(x^{*})>\epsilon\right)\leq\beta
for any minimizers
x^{*}
and
\widehat{x}^{*}
of problems (
MCCO
) and (
1
), respectively. Hence, any minimizer of problem (
1
) constitutes an
\epsilon
-optimal solution to (
MCCO
) with probability at least
1-\beta
.

By Lemma
3.22
, the expected sampling cost of the MLMC estimator amounts to

\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{F}(x))]=n_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}.

By using a similar reasoning as in the proof of Theorem
3.25
, one can show that the product term is of order
\mathcal{O}(\log(\epsilon^{-1})^{T-1})
for Lipschitz continuous integrands and of order
\mathcal{O}(1)
for smooth integrands. Moreover, the number
n_{1}
of scenario trees appearing in the statement of the corollary is of order
\mathcal{O}(\log(\epsilon^{-1})\epsilon^{-2})
. The desired scenario complexity bounds then follow by multiplying these estimates.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs of Section
4

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

We use backward induction on
t
to prove that
\nabla F_{t}^{T}(x)=G_{t}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
t\in[T]
. As for the base case corresponding to
t=T
, fix any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and observe that

\displaystyle\nabla F_{T}^{T}(x)=\nabla\mathbb{E}_{T-1}[f_{T}(\xi_{T},x)]=\mathbb{E}_{T-1}[\nabla_{x}f_{T}(\xi_{T},x)]=G_{T}(x).

Here, the first equality follows from the construction of
F_{T}^{T}(x)
in Definition
C.1
. Next, the gradient may be interchanged with the conditional expectation by the dominated convergence theorem, which applies thanks to Assumptions
3.2
and
3.3
. The resulting expression coincides with the definition of
G_{T}(x)
.

As for the induction step corresponding to any
t\in[T-1]
, assume that
\nabla F_{t+1}^{T}(x)=G_{t+1}(x)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Next, fix an arbitrary
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and observe that

\displaystyle\nabla F_{t}^{T}(x)=

\displaystyle\nabla_{x_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[f_{t}(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}(x))]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[\nabla F_{t+1}^{T}(x)\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}(x))\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[G_{t+1}(x)\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}(x))\right]=G_{t}(x).

Here, the first equality follows from the definition of
F_{t}^{T}
, while the second equality follows from the dominated convergence theorem and the chain rule. The third and the fourth equalities then exploit the induction hypothesis and the definition of
G_{t}
, respectively. The claim ultimately follows because
F(x)=F_{1}^{T}(x)
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

Throughout the proof we fix
x
, and for simplicity we suppress the dependence on
x
in all functions. We then find an upper bound on
\nu_{t}^{2^{t}}
and a recursive formula for
E_{t}
by backward induction on
t
. As for the base case corresponding to
t=T
, the definition of
\widehat{G}_{T}
and Assumption
4.4
imply that

\displaystyle\nu_{T}^{2^{T}}=\mathbb{E}_{T-1}\big[\|\widehat{G}_{T}\|_{2}^{2^{T}}\big]\leq\overline{\nu}_{T}^{2^{T}}\eqqcolon E_{T}<\infty.

As for the induction step corresponding to any
t
with
t+1\in[T]
, we introduce the auxiliary variables

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{s}\coloneqq\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{(2^{s-1})(\rho_{s}+1)\ell}q_{s}(\ell)^{2^{s}-1}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\coloneqq\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{2^{s}(\rho_{s-1}+1)\ell}q_{s}(\ell)^{2^{s}(\rho_{s-1}+1)-1}}\quad\forall s\in[T-1],

(38)

which allows us to express the induction hypothesis compactly as

\displaystyle\nu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\big]\leq E_{t+1}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\max\big\{{\mathcal{S}}_{s},{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\}.

(39)

In order to bound
\nu_{t}^{2^{t}}
, we use the law of total expectation and the definition of
\widehat{G}_{t}
in (
23
) to obtain

\displaystyle\nu_{t}^{2^{t}}=\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\widehat{G}_{t}\|^{2^{t}}_{2}\big]=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}q_{t}(\ell)\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\bigg[\left.\frac{1}{q_{t}(\lambda_{t})^{2^{t}}}\Big\|\widehat{g}_{t}^{\lambda_{t}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\lambda_{t},{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\lambda_{t},{\rm o}}\Big\|^{2^{t}}_{2}\right|\lambda_{t}=\ell\bigg]

(40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{1}{q_{t}(\ell)^{2^{t}-1}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\bigg[\Big\|\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\Big\|^{2^{t}}_{2}\bigg].

In the second line we have eliminated the condition on the event
\lambda_{t}=\ell
, which is allowed because
\lambda_{t}
is independent of all other random objects. Next, we define
\Delta^{\ell}\coloneqq\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}
and

\displaystyle A_{t}^{\ell}\coloneqq\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\Big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\Big)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\Big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\Big)-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\Big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\Big)

to avoid clutter. By the construction of
\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell}
,
\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}
and
\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}
in Definition
4.2
, we thus have

\displaystyle\Delta^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big)\Big[\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big)\Big[\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\Big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big)\Big[\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\,A_{t}^{\ell},

where the second equality is obtained by adding terms that sum to
0
and using the antithetic sample average identity (
17
). Next, we observe that Jensen’s inequality yields
|\sum_{i=1}^{4}a_{i}|^{p}\leq 4^{p-1}\sum_{i=1}^{4}|a_{i}|^{p}
for all
a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}\in\mathbb{R}
and
p\geq 1
. Hence, the
(p=2^{t})
-th conditional moment of
\Delta^{\ell}
obeys the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq{\textstyle 4^{2^{t}-1}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\Big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big)\Big[\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+{\textstyle\frac{4^{2^{t}-1}}{2^{2^{t}}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\Big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big)\Big[\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+{\textstyle\frac{4^{2^{t}-1}}{2^{2^{t}}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\Big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big)\Big[\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+{\textstyle 4^{2^{t}-1}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\;A^{\ell}_{t}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big].

To bound the first three terms above, we recall that
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
. Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\big]

(41)

\displaystyle\leq{\textstyle\frac{4^{2^{t}-1}}{2}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\hskip-5.69054pt+\Big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)\!-\!\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle+{\textstyle\frac{4^{2^{t}-1}}{2^{2^{t}+1}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\hskip-5.69054pt+\Big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)\!-\!\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle+{\textstyle\frac{4^{2^{t}-1}}{2^{2^{t}+1}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\hskip-5.69054pt+\Big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)\!-\!\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle+4^{2^{t}-1}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\,A^{\ell}_{t}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big].

The remainder of the proof proceeds in three steps, where we analyze separately the terms appearing in (
41
). In Step (i), we bound the
2^{t}
-th conditional moment of
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\,A_{t}^{\ell}
, which appears on the last line of (
41
). In Step (ii), we bound the
2^{t+1}
-th conditional moments of the three gradient-difference terms involving
\widehat{H}_{t+1}
. Finally, in Step (iii), we bound the
2^{t+1}
-th conditional moments of the three value-difference terms involving
\widehat{G}_{t+1}
. To complete the induction step, we then substitute all obtained bounds into (
41
) and (
40
).

Step (i)

To bound the
2^{t}
-th conditional moment of
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\,A^{\ell}_{t}
, we first observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\,A^{\ell}_{t}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\big\|A^{\ell}_{t}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big],

(42)

where the inequality follows from the submultiplicativity of the Frobenius norm established in Lemma
A.3
. We emphasize that entry-wise matrix
p
-norms are not submultiplicative for
p>2
. This is our reason for using the Frobenius norm (corresponding to
p=2
) in the definition of the
p
-th conditional moment of
\widehat{G}_{t}(x)
in (
25
).
Next, we bound
\|A^{\ell}_{t}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}
. Jensen’s inequality readily implies that

\|A^{\ell}_{t}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}=\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{d_{t-1}}\big\|A^{\ell}_{t}e_{i}\big\|_{2}^{2}\bigg)^{2^{t-1}}\leq(d_{t-1})^{2^{t-1}-1}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{t-1}}\|A^{\ell}_{t}e_{i}\|_{2}^{2^{t}},

(43)

where
e_{i}
stands for the
i
-th standard basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d_{t-1}}
. By the definition of
A_{t}^{\ell}
, we can rewrite
A_{t}^{\ell}e_{i}
as

\displaystyle\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)\!-\!\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)\!-\!\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)\!-\!\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\!-\!\nabla^{2}_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)

\displaystyle+\!\frac{1}{2}\Big[\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\!-\!\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)\!-\!\nabla^{2}_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]

\displaystyle+\!\frac{1}{2}\Big[\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\!-\!\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)\!-\!\nabla^{2}_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big],

where the equality follows from the antithetic sample average identity (
17
). The norm of
A_{t}^{\ell}e_{i}
thus satisfies

\displaystyle\|A_{t}^{\ell}e_{i}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\;\big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\;-\nabla^{2}_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\;\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\;-\nabla^{2}_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\;\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\;-\nabla^{2}_{x_{t}}f_{t,i}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\;\big(\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\big\|_{2}

by virtue of the triangle inequality. Next, we bound the three norm terms by using the estimate (
24
), which is allowed because the Hessians are Hölder continuous thanks to
Assumption
4.3
. We therefore obtain

\displaystyle\|A_{t}^{\ell}e_{i}\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\;\frac{R_{t}}{\rho_{t}+1}\,\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{\rho_{t}+1}+\frac{R_{t}}{2(\rho_{t}+1)}\,\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{\rho_{t}+1}

\displaystyle+\frac{R_{t}}{2(\rho_{t}+1)}\,\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{\rho_{t}+1}.

Two successive applications of Jensen’s inequality yield
|a+b+c|^{p}\leq 2^{p-1}(|a|^{p}+2^{p-1}(|b|^{p}+|c|^{p}))
for all
a,b,c\in\mathbb{R}
and
p\geq 1
. By raising both sides of the previous inequality to the power
p=2^{t}
we thus find

\displaystyle\|A_{t}^{\ell}e_{i}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{R_{t}}{\rho_{t}+1}\Big)^{2^{t}}2^{2^{t}-1}\;\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\;\quad+\frac{1}{2}\;\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}+\frac{1}{2}\;\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{R_{t}}{\rho_{t}+1}\Big)^{2^{t}}2^{2^{t}-1}(d_{t})^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)-1}\;\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}

\displaystyle\qquad\;\quad+\frac{1}{2}\;\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}+\frac{1}{2}\;\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big],

where the second inequality follows from Lemma
A.2
with
p=2
,
q=2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)
,
m=1
and
n=d_{t}
. Substituting the above bound on
\|A_{t}^{\ell}e_{i}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}
back into (
43
) then results in the estimate

\|A^{\ell}_{t}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\leq\Big(\frac{R_{t}}{\rho_{t}+1}\Big)^{2^{t}}2^{2^{t}-1}(d_{t-1})^{2^{t-1}}\;(d_{t})^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)-1}\bar{A}_{t}^{\ell},

(44)

where we have used the shorthand

\displaystyle\bar{A}_{t}^{\ell}

\displaystyle=\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\;\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}+\frac{1}{2}\;\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}.

Substituting (
44
) into (
42
) and applying the Cauchy-Schwarz and Jensen inequalities then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\big\|A^{\ell}_{t}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big]

(45)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{R_{t}}{\rho_{t}+1}\Big)^{2^{t}}2^{2^{t}-1}(d_{t-1})^{2^{t-1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)-1}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\bar{A}_{t}^{\ell}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{R_{t}}{\rho_{t}+1}\Big)^{2^{t}}2^{2^{t}-1}(d_{t-1})^{2^{t-1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)-1}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big(\bar{A}_{t}^{\ell}\big)^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Note that
\widehat{G}_{t+1}
and
\widehat{H}_{t+1}
are constructed from the same sets of samples, which implies that
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]
and
\bar{A}_{t}^{\ell}
are dependent.
To further simplify the bound in (
45
), we use the Minkowski inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big(\bar{A}_{t}^{\ell}\big)^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(d_{t})^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg).

Here, the second inequality follows from Lemma
A.2
with
p=2^{t}(\rho_{t}+1)
,
q=2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)
,
m=1
and
n=d_{t}
.
By using Lemma
A.4
with
p=2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)
and
n=2^{\ell}
or
n=2^{\ell-1}
, we further obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big(\bar{A}_{t}^{\ell}\big)^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(d_{t})^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\frac{B_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{(2^{t-1})(\rho_{t}+1)\ell}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+(d_{t})^{\frac{1}{2}}\;\frac{B_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{(2^{t-1)}(\rho_{t}+1)(\ell-1)}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\Big(1+2^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big)\,(d_{t})^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{(2^{t-1})(\rho_{t}+1)\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Substituting the resulting expression into (
45
) and combining it with (
42
) finally yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]A^{\ell}_{t}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\frac{R_{t}}{\rho_{t}+1}\Big)^{2^{t}}2^{2^{t}-1}(d_{t-1})^{2^{t-1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)-1}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\Big(1+2^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big)(d_{t})^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\frac{B_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{(2^{t-1})(\rho_{t}+1)\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

We have thus established an upper bound on the last term in (
41
), which depends on conditional moments of
\widehat{G}_{t+1}
and
\widehat{H}_{t+1}
and decays geometrically with
\ell
. This concludes Step (i).

Step (ii)

To bound the gradient difference terms in (
41
) depending on
\widehat{H}_{t+1}
, we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\leq S_{t}^{2^{t+1}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq S_{t}^{2^{t+1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]\leq S_{t}^{2^{t+1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big],

where the first inequality exploits Assumption
3.3
, and the second inequality follows from Lemma
A.2
with
p=2
,
q=2^{t+1}
,
m=1
and
n=d_{t}
. The third inequality follows from the law of total expectation and from Lemma
A.4
with
p=2^{t+1}
and
n=2^{\ell}
. A similar reasoning implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq S_{t}^{2^{t+1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}(\ell-1)}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq S_{t}^{2^{t+1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}(\ell-1)}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big].

As the empirical averages here are taken only over samples with even or odd indices, we set
n=2^{\ell-1}
when applying Lemma
A.4
. In summary, we have found upper bounds on the gradient difference terms in (
41
), which depend on conditional moments of
\widehat{H}_{t+1}
and decay geometrically with
\ell
. This concludes Step (ii).

Step (iii)

To bound the terms in (
41
) depending on
\widehat{G}_{t+1}
, we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(d_{t}d)^{2^{t}-1}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\Big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(d_{t}d)^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(d_{t}d)^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big],

where the first inequality follows from Lemma
A.2
with
p=2
,
q=2^{t+1}
,
m=d_{t}
and
n=d
, while the second inequality follows from the law of total expectation and from Lemma
A.4
with
p=2^{t+1}
and
n=2^{\ell}
. The third inequality follows from Jensen’s inequality (or from Lemma
A.2
with
p=2
and
q=2^{t+1}
).
A similar reasoning involving Lemma
A.4
with
p=2^{t+1}
and
n=2^{\ell-1}
implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(d_{t}d)^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}(\ell-1)}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\big[\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(d_{t}d)^{2^{t}-1}\frac{B_{2^{t+1}}}{2^{2^{t}(\ell-1)}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big].

These bounds depend on conditional moments of
\widehat{G}_{t+1}
and decay geometrically with
\ell
, completing Step (iii).

Having analyzed all terms in (
41
) in Steps (i), (ii) and (iii), we are now ready to combine the resulting estimates to a bound on the conditional moments of
\Delta^{\ell}
. For notational convenience, we define the constants

\displaystyle Y_{t}\coloneqq

\displaystyle 3\cdot 2^{2^{t+1}-3}(d_{t}d)^{2^{t}-1}B_{2^{t+1}},

\displaystyle Y^{\prime}_{t}\coloneqq

\displaystyle 3\cdot 2^{2^{t+1}-3}S_{t}^{2^{t+1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t}-1}B_{2^{t+1}},

\displaystyle Y^{\prime\prime}_{t}\coloneqq

\displaystyle 2^{2^{t+1}+2^{t}-3}\Big(\frac{R_{t}}{\rho_{t}+1}\Big)^{2^{t}}(d_{t-1})^{2^{t-1}}(d_{t})^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(1+2^{2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big)B_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{\frac{1}{2}},

which are independent of
\ell
. Substituting the bounds from Steps (i), (ii) and (iii) into (
41
) then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Y_{t}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]+\frac{Y_{t}^{\prime}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]

(47)

\displaystyle+\frac{Y_{t}^{\prime\prime}}{2^{(2^{t-1})(\rho_{t}+1)\ell}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2^{t+1}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Next, we bound the three conditional expectations appearing in (
47
). By Lemma
3.19
, under Assumption
3.3
, and using the fact that
2^{s-1}(\rho_{s}+1)\leq 2^{s}
, the second conditional expectation satisfies

\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\|_{2^{t+1}}^{2^{t+1}}\big]\leq D_{t+1}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{2^{s}\ell}q_{s}(\ell)^{2^{s}-1}}\bigg)\leq D_{t+1}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}{\mathcal{S}}_{s},

where

D_{t+1}=\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2^{T}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{3S_{s}}{2}\bigg)^{2^{s}}d_{s}^{2^{s}-1}B_{2^{s+1}}\bigg)

and
{\mathcal{S}}_{s}
is defined in (
38
). By adapting Lemma
3.19
in the obvious way, one can further show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}\big\|_{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}^{2^{t+1}(\rho_{t}+1)}\Big]\leq D_{t+1}^{\prime}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime},

where

\displaystyle D_{t+1}^{\prime}\coloneqq\overline{\mu}_{T}^{2^{T}(\rho_{T-1}+1)}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\bigg(\bigg(\frac{3S_{s}}{2}\bigg)^{2^{s}(\rho_{s-1}+1)}d_{s}^{2^{s}(\rho_{s-1}+1)-1}B_{2^{s+1}(\rho_{s-1}+1)}\bigg)

and
{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}
is defined in (
38
). Moreover, by the induction hypothesis, the conditional moment of
\widehat{G}_{t+1}
in (
47
) satisfies the bound (
39
). Substituting all of these conditional moment bounds into (
47
) then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Y_{t}E_{t+1}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\max\big\{{\mathcal{S}}_{s},{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\}+\frac{Y^{\prime}_{t}D_{t+1}}{2^{2^{t}\ell}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}{\mathcal{S}}_{s}

\displaystyle+\frac{Y_{t}^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{E_{t+1}{D_{t+1}^{\prime}}}}{2^{(2^{t-1})(\rho_{t}+1)\ell}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\max\big\{{\mathcal{S}}_{s},{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big({\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{E_{t}}{2^{(2^{t-1})(\rho_{t}+1)\ell}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\max\big\{{\mathcal{S}}_{s},{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\},

where the second inequality follows from the trivial observations that
2^{t-1}(\rho_{t}+1)\leq 2^{t}
and that both
{\mathcal{S}}_{s}
as well as
{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}
are dominated by
\max\{{\mathcal{S}}_{s},{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\}
. The inequality also exploits the (recursive) definition of
E_{t}
as

E_{t}\coloneqq Y_{t}E_{t+1}+Y^{\prime}_{t}D_{t+1}+Y_{t}^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{E_{t+1}{D_{t+1}^{\prime}}}.

Replacing the conditional moment
\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|_{2}^{2^{t}}]
in (
40
) with the above bound finally yields

\displaystyle\nu_{t}^{2^{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\big[\|\Delta^{\ell}\|^{2^{t}}_{2}\big]}{q_{t}(\ell)^{2^{t}-1}}\leq E_{t}\,{\mathcal{S}}_{t}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\max\big\{{\mathcal{S}}_{s},{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\}\leq E_{t}\prod_{s=t}^{T-1}\max\big\{{\mathcal{S}}_{s},{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\}.

This completes the induction step. By their recursive definition and thanks to Assumption
4.4
, the constants
E_{t}
,
t\in[T-1]
, are finite whenever
E_{T}
is finite. Hence, the claim follows.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

We simplify notation by suppressing the dependence on
x
for all functions of
x
in this proof. In the following we will show that the conditional bias of the estimator
\widehat{G}_{t}(x)
obeys the estimate

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{G}_{t}]-G_{t}\big\|_{2}

(48)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=t}^{T-1}L_{[t:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\nu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}+\sum_{k=s+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[s+1:k-1]}S_{k}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[(\mu_{k+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{k}+1}}\bigg)

for all
t\in[T]
. As
\mathbb{E}[\widehat{G}]=\mathbb{E}[\widehat{G}_{1}]
, the claim then follows immediately. We prove (
48
) by backward induction on
t
. As for the base case corresponding to
t=T
, note that
\widehat{G}_{T}
constitutes an unbiased estimator for
G_{T}
. Thus, the left hand side of (
48
) evaluates to
0
, and the claim holds trivially (in fact, the right hand side of (
48
) vanishes, too). As for the induction step corresponding to any
t
with
t+1\in[T]
, assume that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]-G_{t+1}\big\|_{2}\leq\!\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\nu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}\!+\!\sum_{k=s+1}^{T-1}\!\frac{L_{[s+1:k-1]}S_{k}\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\mu_{k+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{k}+1}}\bigg).

In addition, note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{G}_{t}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[\frac{1}{q_{t}(\lambda_{t})}\left(\widehat{g}_{t}^{\lambda_{t}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\lambda_{t},{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\lambda_{t},{\rm o}}\right)\right]=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\left[\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}\right]

(49)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{g}_{t}^{M_{t}}]=\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big],

where the first equality follows from the definition of
\widehat{G}_{t}
in (
23
), and the second uses the law of total expectation along with the independence of
\lambda_{t}
from all other random objects. The third equality holds because
\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm e}}
and
\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell,{\rm o}}
share the distribution of
\widehat{g}_{t}^{\ell-1}
conditional on
\mathcal{F}_{t}
for all
\ell\in[M_{t}]
and because
\widehat{g}_{t}^{0,{\rm e}}=\widehat{g}_{t}^{0,{\rm o}}=0
. Finally, the fourth equality follows from Definition
4.2
. Next, we introduce the shorthands

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\left(\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\left(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}\right)\right)\Big]\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\Big(\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]-G_{t+1}\Big)\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\left(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}\right)\Big]\Big\|_{2}

to simplify notation. Then, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{G}_{t}]-G_{t}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)\Big]-\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[G_{t+1}\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}\big)\Big]\Big\|_{2}

(50)

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}\big)\Big]\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\Big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}\big)-G_{t+1}\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\left(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}\right)\Big]\Big\|_{2}\!=A_{1}\!+A_{2},

where the first equality exploits (
49
) and the definition of
G_{t}
in (
22
). The second equality follows from the law of total expectation and the elementary identity
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\cdot]]=\mathbb{E}_{t}[\cdot]
. Next, we separately bound the terms
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
. As for
A_{1}
, we use the Cauchy-Schwarz and Jensen inequalities to obtain

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}\Big]\big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\big(\xi_{t},\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big)-\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\left(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}\right)\big\|_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq S_{t}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}\big\|\,\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-F_{t+1}^{T}\big\|_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq S_{t}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-F_{t+1}^{T}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}
