\displaystyle=S_{t}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-F_{t+1}^{T}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Here, the second inequality follows from the smoothness of the integrand
f_{t}
, as stipulated in Assumption
3.3
. Note that we have also removed the first sample average
\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\cdot]
. This is permissible because the second norm term does not depend on
\xi_{t+1}
and because the underlying samples are independent and identically distributed conditional on the information available at time
t-1
. The third inequality and the equality follow from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and from a standard bias-variance decomposition of the mean squared error.
By Lemma
C.6
, the bias term admits the following upper bound.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-F_{t+1}^{T}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]

In addition, the variance term satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]=2^{-M_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{H}_{t+1}-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]\leq 2^{-M_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}],

where the equality holds because the variance of the sample average
\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{t}^{M_{t}}[\widehat{H}_{t+1}]
equals the variance of
\widehat{H}_{t+1}
divided by the sample size
2^{M_{t}}
. The inequality follows from the observation that the variance of
\widehat{H}_{t+1}
is bounded below by its second moment and from the definition of
\mu_{t+1}^{2}
in (
19
). Substituting the bounds on these mean and the variance terms into the above bound on
A_{1}
then yields

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle\leq S_{t}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\widehat{G}_{t+1}\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}]}{2^{M_{t}}}\Bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq S_{t}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\nu_{t+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}\Bigg),

where the second inequality holds because of the definition of
\nu_{t+1}^{2}
in (
25
) and because
(a^{2}+b^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq|a|+|b|
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
. The Minkowski and Jensen inequalities further imply that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\bigg[\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}^{2}S_{s}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]^{2}}{2^{2M_{s}+2}}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[(\mu_{s+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}+1}}.

Using this inequality to simplify that above bound on
A_{1}
yields

\displaystyle A_{1}\leq S_{t}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\nu_{t+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[(\mu_{s+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}+1}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}\bigg).

As for the term
A_{2}
, we use the Cauchy-Schwarz and Jensen inequalities to obtain

\displaystyle A_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\nabla_{x_{t}}f_{t}\left(\xi_{t},F_{t+1}^{T}\right)\big\|_{2}\,\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]-G_{t+1}\big\|_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq L_{t}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\Big[\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t}[\widehat{G}_{t+1}]-G_{t+1}\big\|_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\leq L_{t}\;\mathbb{E}_{t-1}\bigg[\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t}[\nu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}+\bigg(\sum_{k=s+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[s+1:k-1]}S_{k}\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\mu_{k+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{k}+1}}\bigg)\bigg)\bigg],

where the second inequality follows from Assumption
3.2
, and the third inequality exploits the induction hypothesis. Jensen’s inequality together with the law of total expectations further yields

\displaystyle A_{2}\leq L_{t}\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\nu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}+\bigg(\sum_{k=s+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[s+1:k-1]}S_{k}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[(\mu_{k+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{k}+1}}\bigg)\bigg).

Combining the bounds on
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
with (
50
) and noting that
L_{[t:t-1]}=1
, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\widehat{G}_{t}]-G_{t}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq S_{t}\,\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\nu_{t+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}+\bigg(\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[(\mu_{s+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\;\quad+L_{t}\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\nu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}+\bigg(\sum_{k=s+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[s+1:k-1]}S_{k}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[(\mu_{k+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{k}+1}}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{s=t}^{T-1}L_{[t:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\nu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[\mu_{s+1}^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}/2}}+\sum_{k=s+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[s+1:k-1]}S_{k}\mathbb{E}_{t-1}[(\mu_{k+1}^{2})^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{k}+1}}\bigg).

This completes the induction step, and thus the claim follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.9
.

To bound the root mean squared error of
\widehat{G}(x)
by
\epsilon
, it suffices to bound both the bias and the standard deviation of
\widehat{G}(x)
by
\epsilon/\sqrt{2}
for any
x\in\mathcal{X}
. As for the bias, Lemma
4.6
implies that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{G}(x)]-G_{1}(x)\big\|_{2}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}L_{[t-1]}S_{t}\mathbb{E}[\nu_{t+1}^{2}(x)]^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}[\mu_{t+1}^{2}(x)]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}+\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}[(\mu_{s+1}^{2}(x))^{2}]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}L_{[t-1]}S_{t}\mathbb{E}\big[\nu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}(x)\big]^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}\bigg(\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[\mu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}(x)\big]^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}+\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}\mathbb{E}\big[(\mu_{s+1}^{2^{s+1}}(x))^{2}\big]^{\frac{1}{2^{s+1}}}}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\bigg),

where the last expression follows from repeated application of Jensen’s inequality and from the definitions of the conditional moments
\mu_{t}^{p}(x)
and
\nu_{t}^{p}(x)
in (
19
) and (
25
), respectively. Lemma
4.5
further implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\nu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}(x)\big]^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}

\displaystyle\leq E_{t+1}^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\max\big\{{\mathcal{S}}_{s},{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\}^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}},

where the auxiliary variables
{\mathcal{S}}_{s}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}
are defined as in (
38
) in the proof of Lemma
4.5
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{s}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{1}{2^{(2^{s-1})(\rho_{s}+1)\ell}q_{s}(\ell)^{2^{s}-1}}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{s}}\frac{Z_{s}^{2^{s}-1}}{2^{(2^{s-1})(\rho_{s}+1)\ell}(1-r_{s})^{(2^{s}-1)\ell}}.

Here, the second equality holds because
q_{s}(\ell)=(1-r_{s})^{\ell}/Z_{s}
, and
Z_{s}\coloneqq(1-(1-r_{s})^{M_{s}+1})/r_{s}
is shorthand for the normalization constant of the truncated geometric distribution
\mathrm{Geo}(r_{s}|M_{s})
. Note that
Z_{s}\leq 1/r_{s}
. The first upper bound on
r_{s}
in Assumption
4.8
thus ensures that
{\mathcal{S}}_{s}
remains bounded as
M_{s}
tends to infinity. Consequently, there exists a constant
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{s}\geq{\mathcal{S}}_{s}
that is independent of
M_{s}
. Similarly, one can show that there exists a constant
\bar{{\mathcal{S}}}_{s}^{\prime}\geq{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}
that is independent of
M_{s}
. In summary, we may then conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\nu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}(x)\big]^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}

\displaystyle\leq E_{t+1}^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\max\big\{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{s},\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\}^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}

uniformly across all
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
M_{s}
,
s=t+1,\ldots,T-1
. As
\rho_{s}\leq 1
, Lemma
3.19
further implies that

\displaystyle\mu_{t+1}^{2^{t+1}}(x)\leq D_{t+1}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{s}

uniformly across all
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
M_{s}
,
s=t+1,\ldots,T-1
. Combining the above estimates then yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{G}(x)]-G_{1}(x)\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}L_{[t-1]}S_{t}E_{t+1}^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}\Big(\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\textstyle\max\big\{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{s},\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\prime}\big\}^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\frac{D_{t+1}^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}\prod_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{s}^{\frac{1}{2^{t+1}}}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}+\sum_{s=t+1}^{T-1}\frac{L_{[t+1:s-1]}S_{s}D_{s+1}^{\frac{1}{2^{s}}}\prod_{s^{\prime}=s+1}^{T-1}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{s^{\prime}}^{\frac{1}{2^{s}}}}{2^{M_{s}+1}}\bigg).

By construction, all constants in the resulting bound are independent of the truncation points. Since
M_{s}\geq 0
, we can lower bound the denominator
2^{M_{s}+1}
by
2^{M_{s}/2}
and introduce auxiliary constants
W_{t}^{\prime}\geq 0
that are independent of the truncation points to obtain a simpler but relaxed bias bound of the form

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[\widehat{G}(x)]-G_{1}(x)\|_{2}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{W_{t}^{\prime}}{2^{M_{t}/2}}.

Note that
W_{t}^{\prime}
absorbs all dependencies of the bias on the Lipschitz, smoothness and Hölder constants, as well as on the dimensions, moment bounds and rate parameters. Setting
W_{t}=W^{\prime}_{t}\sqrt{2}(T-1)
, it is now evident that the bias drops below
\epsilon/\sqrt{2}
uniformly across all
x\in\mathcal{X}
if
M_{t}=\lceil 2\log_{2}(W_{t}/\epsilon)\rceil
for all
t\in[T-1]
.

Next, we show that the variance of the MLMC gradient estimator is bounded by
\epsilon^{2}/2
. From the first part of the proof we know that
\nu_{1}^{2}(x)
is bounded above by
E_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\max\{\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{t},\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{t}^{\prime}\}
uniformly across all
x\in\mathcal{X}
. Thus, both
\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}\nu_{1}^{2}(x)
and
n_{1}
are finite. Consequently, we may conclude that

\mathbb{V}(\widehat{G}(x))\leq\frac{\nu_{1}^{2}(x)}{n_{1}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2}

uniformly across all
x\in\mathcal{X}
, where the two inequalities follow from (
26
) and the definition of
n_{1}
.

Finally, the expected sampling cost of the MLMC gradient estimator is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{G}(x))]=n_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}q_{t}(\ell)2^{\ell}=n_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{M_{t}}\frac{2^{\ell}(1-r_{t})^{\ell}}{Z_{t}}=n_{1}\prod_{t=1}^{T-1}\frac{1-(2-2r_{t})^{M_{t}+1}}{Z_{t}(2r_{t}-1)},

where the first equality can be proved as in Lemma
3.22
. As
2^{-1}<r_{t}<1
by virtue of Assumption
4.8
, the resulting expression remains bounded as
M_{t}
tends to infinity.

In summary, the above reasoning implies that the root mean squared error of the MLMC gradient estimator is bounded by
\epsilon
uniformly across all
x\in\mathcal{X}
if
n_{1}=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2})
and
M_{t}=\mathcal{O}(\log(\epsilon^{-1}))
for all
t\in[T-1]
, in which case the expected sampling cost satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{C}(\widehat{G}(x))]\leq\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-2})
. Hence, the claim follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.11
.

Under the stated assumptions, Lemmas
4.5
,
4.6
, and
4.7
, together with Theorem
4.9
, are applicable. It is therefore easy to verify that the claim is a direct consequence of
[
40
, Theorem C.1]
.
∎
